L2345: Yasmin Choudhry, M.D.
L2346: BLA 761068
L2347: Burosumab
L2348: BPI-SF Item 3 (pain intensity); WOMAC (stiffness; physical function)
L2349: *However, the BFI-SF “pain interference score” and WOMAC “stiffness score” were not significantly different.*
L2350: *These results should also be interpreted with caution due to the small sample size and differences in numbers per group (6 vs 14). Additionally, the Applicant did not provide justification for the cutoffs for mild, moderate and severe pain.*
L2351: **Ability to detect change:** Evidence that an instrument can identify differences in scores over time in individuals or groups who have changed with respect to the concept.
L2352: The Applicant conducted responsiveness analyses to determine the extent to which BPI-SF and WOMAC scores were able to detect changes in patients when their clinical status has improved between baseline and week 24, using the data from the open-label extension study only (UX023-CL203). Improved and not improved groups were based on a 6MWT and TUG test (Participants showing +/- &gt;0.5SD change in each of these tests).
L2353: *Reviewer comments: Since there were very limited numbers in the ‘improved’ group using both the 6MWT definition (n=3) and TUG definition (n=6), the Applicant chose not to present figures for this analysis as the sample did not allow inference to be drawn regarding ability to detect change.*
L2354: ## 7 INTERPRETATION OF SCORES
L2355: For BFI-SF Item 3 (worst pain) and WOMAC Physical Functioning and Stiffness domains, anchor- and distribution-based approaches were used to define the magnitude of change that can be considered meaningful. The Applicant concluded that the estimates for thresholds for meaningful change based on anchor- and distribution-based methods for the WOMAC were as follows:
L2356: - The BPI-SF Item 3 (worst pain) estimates (anchor-based, ½ SD, SEM) were in the range of 0.91-1.62, with a mean percentage change from baseline of -23.65%
- The WOMAC Stiffness domain ranged from 7.16-16.67; and the Physical functioning domain ranged from 4.10-13.6
L2357: *Reviewer comments: The anchor-based estimates for these variables includes large standard deviations, most likely due to the small samples change between baseline and week 24 for stiffness (-16.67 (SD:21.48)) and physical functioning (13.63 (SD: 21.82)). The Applicant proposed to use the upper end of the range. The clinical meaningfulness of the proposed ranges is not known.*
L2358: The Applicant provided cumulative distribution functions (CDF) curves based on change from baseline and percent change from baseline to Week 24 in mean BPI Worst Pain score, WOMAC
L2359: ## Clinical Outcome Assessment Review
L2360: Yasmin Choudhry, M.D.
L2361: BLA 761068
L2362: Burosumab
L2363: BPI-SF Item 3 (pain intensity); WOMAC (stiffness; physical function)
L2364: Stiffness and Physical Function scores were plotted by treatment groups. See clinical study report for Study UXO23-CL303 (page 1216/2366).
L2365: The BPI Worst Pain responder analyses were based on the following definitions:
L2366: - Subjects with at least 15% decrease from baseline
- Subjects with at least 30% decrease from baseline
L2367: The current submission included the following minimum perceptible clinical improvement (MPCI) for the WOMAC domains based on published literature (the WOMAC User Guide (Bellamy 2012)): WOMAC Stiffness: 10.0 nu; and WOMAC Physical Functioning: 9.3 nu.
L2368: The Applicant concluded that the psychometric validation of the WOMAC in patients with XLH found similar MPCI values. For each of the WOMAC domains, responders were defined as subjects with a decrease from baseline greater than or equal to the corresponding MPCI.
L2369: *Reviewer comments: The clinical meaningfulness of the MPCI proposed ranges is not known.*
L2370: *Of note, in Study UX023-CL303, the Patient Global Impression of Severity (PGI-S) / Patient Global Impression of Improvement (PGI-I) were also utilized. The 4-point PGI-S was administered at the Baseline visit; and the PGI-I (7-point categorical scale ranging from 'very much better' to 'very much worse') was administered at Weeks 12, 24, 36, and 48.*
L2371: *The Applicant was requested (IR dated November 30, 2017) to provide anchor-based CDF figures with the PGI-I. The Applicant responded (in SDN 23) that "they were unable to construct the requested plots for the WOMAC Stiffness, WOMAC Physical Function, and BPI Worst Pain score demonstrating a change from baseline in either patient global impression of improvement (PGI-I) scales as the PGI-S was only administered at baseline, while the PGI-I was administered at week 12 and week 24." Despite this statement, the Applicant did provide CDF and PDF plots. Upon review, the CDF plots based on the 7 categories of the PGI-I (i.e., from very much better, much better, a little better, no change, a little worse, much worse, and very much worse) demonstrate most categories are overlapping except for the "much better" category. However, the distribution of patients across the categories is poor and there is a lack of precision in the estimate of the clinically meaningful threshold due to the small number of patients in the much better category. Therefore, the CDFs are difficult to interpret.*
L2372: *Based on the information reviewed, we believe that the thresholds for meaningful change in the instruments for this target population is unknown.*
L2373: ## 8 LANGUAGE TRANSLATION AND CULTURAL ADAPTATION
L2374: The evidence dossier states that for all languages included in the clinical trial(s), a fully translated and linguistically validated version of the BPI-SF and WOMAC Version LK3.1 was used.
L2375: *Reviewer comments: The BPI has been translated into dozens of languages, and it is widely used in both research and clinical settings. The BPI is available in two formats: the BPI short form, which is used for clinical trials and is the version used for the foreign-language translations.*
L2376: *Based on the publications cited in the user guide it appears the following languages are available: Spanish; Taiwanese; Russian; Italian; Norwegian; Greek; Canadian French; French; German; and Japanese Chinese Korean.*
L2377: *The user guide does not specify what process was used to translate the BPI-SF.*
L2378: *The WOMAC Version LK3.1 has been translated and linguistically validated in French-Canadian. The Phase 3 clinical trial (UX023-CL303) was conducted at sites in countries including the USA, UK, Ireland, Japan (Japanese), Canada (French Canadian), France (French), Denmark (Danish), Italy (Italian) and Korea (Korean). It is unclear whether the WOMAC was appropriately translated into the relevant languages (Japanese, Danish, Korean, Italian etc.)*

L2479: Figure 1: Functions of FGF23. Panel A illustrates the renal mechanism. FGF23 acts on the Proximal Convoluted Tubule (PCT) and Distal Tubule (DT). In the PCT, FGF23 inhibits Npt2a (reducing phosphate reabsorption) and Cyp27b1 (reducing 1,25(OH)2D production). In the DT, FGF23 activates FGFR/Klotho complexes, leading to increased Cyp24a1 expression (increasing 1,25(OH)2D catabolism). Panel B illustrates extrarenal targets, showing FGF23 acting on the Parathyroid Gland (PT), Heart, Brain, Thymus, Spleen, Kidney, and Bone.
L2480: Figure 2.6.2.1.3.1.1 is a line graph showing the effect of KRN23 IV on Serum Phosphorus Levels in Adult Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study No. (b) (4) 07190). The Y-axis represents Phosphate (mg/dL) and the X-axis represents Day (0 to 10). The graph displays data for the Vehicle group and six dose groups: 0.001 mg/kg, 0.003 mg/kg, 0.01 mg/kg, 0.03 mg/kg, 0.1 mg/kg, and 0.3 mg/kg. The Vehicle group shows a peak phosphate level around Day 2 (approximately 7.5 mg/dL). Dose groups 0.001 mg/kg and 0.003 mg/kg also show peaks around Day 2 (approximately 6.5 mg/dL and 6.8 mg/dL, respectively). Dose groups 0.01 mg/kg, 0.03 mg/kg, 0.1 mg/kg, and 0.3 mg/kg show lower peak levels (around 5.5 mg/dL to 6.0 mg/dL) and generally maintain levels below the Vehicle group after Day 2. Statistical significance is indicated by asterisks (*P<0.05, **P<0.01) comparing the dose groups to the Vehicle group at specific time points.
L2481: Figure 2.6.2.1.3.1.2 is a line graph showing the effect of KRN23 IV on Serum 1,25(OH)2D Levels in Adult Cynomolgus Monkeys (Study No. (b) (4) 07190). The Y-axis represents 1,25(OH)2D (ng/mL) and the X-axis represents Day (0 to 10). The graph displays data for the Vehicle group and six dose groups: 0.001 mg/kg, 0.003 mg/kg, 0.01 mg/kg, 0.03 mg/kg, 0.1 mg/kg, and 0.3 mg/kg. The Vehicle group shows a sharp peak in 1,25(OH)2D levels around Day 1 (approximately 1800 ng/mL). Dose groups 0.001 mg/kg and 0.003 mg/kg also show peaks around Day 1 (approximately 1200 ng/mL and 1300 ng/mL, respectively). Dose groups 0.01 mg/kg, 0.03 mg/kg, 0.1 mg/kg, and 0.3 mg/kg show lower peak levels (around 500 ng/mL to 600 ng/mL) and generally maintain levels below the Vehicle group after Day 1. Statistical significance is indicated by double asterisks (**P<0.01) comparing the dose groups to the Vehicle group at specific time points.

L2486: Figure 7: In vitro ADCC assay showing Fluorescence intensity versus Burosumab concentration (ng/mL). The graph displays four conditions: Positive control (Product A IgG1), Burosumab + FGF23 (12.5 ng/mL), Burosumab + FGF23 (2.5 ng/mL), and Burosumab - FGF23. The positive control shows high fluorescence intensity (around 38,000) across the tested range (0.01 to 100 ng/mL). The Burosumab + FGF23 (12.5 ng/mL) condition shows a dose-dependent increase in fluorescence intensity, reaching approximately 20,000 at 100 ng/mL. The Burosumab + FGF23 (2.5 ng/mL) and Burosumab - FGF23 conditions show minimal or no increase in fluorescence intensity, remaining close to the baseline level (around 17,000).

L2498: Figure 19 displays three plots illustrating the exposure-response relationship for serum phosphorus concentrations based on individual predicted serum burosumab concentrations in subjects with XLH.
(A) Pediatric subjects: Shows Serum Phosphorus Concentration (mg/dL) versus Serum Burosumab Concentration (mcg/mL). The plot includes observed data points (Observations) and predicted curves for the 10th percentile (P10), 50th percentile (P50), and 90th percentile (P90) of the population. The P50 curve shows a peak around 5 mcg/mL.
(B) Adult subjects: Shows Serum Phosphorus Concentration (mg/dL) versus Serum Burosumab Concentration (mcg/mL). The plot includes observed data points (Observations) and predicted curves for P10, P50, and P90. The P50 curve shows a peak around 5 mcg/mL.
(C) Pediatric and adult subjects: Shows Serum Phosphorus Change from Baseline (mg/dL) versus Individual Predicted Burosumab Concentration (ng/mL). This plot displays individual data points for Adult and Pediatric subjects, along with a fitted curve representing the population response. The curve shows a sigmoidal relationship, indicating that higher predicted burosumab concentrations lead to greater normalization of serum phosphorus levels.

L2507: Schematic representation of the Structural PK Model for Burosumab. The model shows a central compartment (V/F) receiving input from SC of KRN23 (absorption rate Ka). The central compartment is connected to clearance (CL/F). Both V/F and CL/F are scaled by allometric functions based on body weight (WT/70). The scaling factors are (WT/70)^VcWT for V/F and (WT/70)^CLWT for CL/F. BetweenSubject Variability (BSV) is indicated below the CL/F path.

Error bars represent standard deviation (SD).
A grey shaded area indicates the Upper Limit of Normal (ULN) range, approximately 297 to 385 U/L.
The Q2 regimen (blue line) shows ALP levels starting around 450 U/L at Week 0, decreasing slightly to around 380 U/L by Week 8, and then remaining relatively stable between 350 U/L and 400 U/L throughout the study period (Weeks 16 to 88).
The Q4 regimen (green line) shows ALP levels starting around 450 U/L at Week 0, decreasing to around 400 U/L by Week 8, and then remaining stable between 350 U/L and 400 U/L throughout the study period (Weeks 16 to 88).
Both regimens maintain ALP levels significantly above the ULN range for the majority of the study duration.

L2524: Figure 45 Study CL303: Study Schema
The study schema illustrates a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial for adults with X-linked hypophosphatemia (XLH).
Eligibility Criteria: Adults 18-65 years with XLH; Serum P <2.5 mg/dL; Measurable bone/joint pain (BPI).
Study Flow:
SCREENING leads to RANDOMIZATION.
RANDOMIZATION assigns 60 participants to each arm:
Arm 1 (Active Treatment): KRN23 (1 mg/kg) for 96 weeks.
Arm 2 (Placebo Arm): Placebo for 24 weeks, followed by KRN23 (1 mg/kg) for 72 weeks.
Timeline (Weeks 0 to 96):
Weeks 0-24: Placebo-controlled Treatment Period.
Weeks 24-48: Treatment Continuation Period.
Weeks 48-96: Treatment Extension Period.
Analysis Points:
Week 24 Primary Analysis
Week 48 Analysis
Week 96 Final Analysis
Both arms proceed to an Extension Study after Week 96.
L2525: Line graph showing Mean Serum Phosphorus (mg/dL) over 48 weeks for two regimens: Placebo -> KRN23 (N=66) and KRN23 -> KRN23 (N=60). The graph is divided into a Double-Blinded Period (Weeks 0-24) and an Open-Label Period (Weeks 24-48). In the Double-Blinded Period, the KRN23 group shows a sharp increase in serum phosphorus from approximately 2.0 mg/dL to a peak around 3.5 mg/dL by Week 6, followed by a slight decrease. The Placebo group remains stable around 2.0 mg/dL. In the Open-Label Period, the KRN23 group stabilizes around 3.0 mg/dL, while the Placebo group increases to around 3.5 mg/dL. A dotted line indicates the LLN (Lower Limit of Normal) in adults at 2.5 mg/dL.
L2526: Line graph showing Mean Serum Phosphorus (mg/dL) over 48 weeks for Study CL303, divided into a Double-Blinded Period (Weeks 0-24) and an Open-Label Period (Weeks 24-48). The Y-axis ranges from 1.5 to 5.0 mg/dL. A dotted line indicates the LLN (Lower Limit of Normal) in adults at 2.5 mg/dL.
The graph compares two regimens: Placebo \to KRN23 (N=66, blue line) and KRN23 \to KRN23 (N=68, green line). Both groups start around 2.0 mg/dL at Week 0.
Double-Blinded Period (Weeks 0-24):
Placebo \to KRN23: Serum phosphorus remains stable around 2.0 mg/dL.
KRN23 \to KRN23: Serum phosphorus rises sharply to approximately 3.0 mg/dL by Week 4, then gradually decreases, stabilizing around 2.5 mg/dL by Week 24.
Open-Label Period (Weeks 24-48):
Placebo \to KRN23: Serum phosphorus rises sharply to approximately 3.0 mg/dL by Week 28, then gradually decreases, stabilizing around 2.5 mg/dL by Week 48.
KRN23 \to KRN23: Serum phosphorus remains stable around 2.5 mg/dL.
Legend:
Regimen	Placebo \to KRN23	KRN23 \to KRN23
Placebo	Blue line with circle marker	Green line with circle marker
KRN23	Blue line with square marker	Green line with square marker
L2527: Line graph showing Mean (SD) serum 1,25(OH)2D level (pg/mL) over time (Weeks) for Placebo (N=66) and KRN23 (N=68) groups in Study CL303. The Y-axis is Serum 1,25(OH)2D level (pg/mL) ranging from 0 to 200. The X-axis is Week, showing time points 0, 1, 2, 4, 20, 21, 22. The Placebo group (blue circles) shows a peak around Week 1 (approx. 40 pg/mL) and then stabilizes around 30-40 pg/mL. The KRN23 group (green squares) shows a peak around Week 1 (approx. 120 pg/mL) and then rapidly declines, stabilizing around 50-60 pg/mL by Week 4. Both groups stabilize around 40-50 pg/mL by Week 20-22. Dotted lines indicate the normal range (18-72 pg/mL).
